  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n**Phase 1 (T1-T3) is the safety net.** T1 is a read-only audit \u2014 trace the actual code paths, don't trust the plan's flow descriptions blindly. Do not proceed to Phase 2 until T3 tests are green.\n\n**Phase 2 (T4-T6) is mechanical but order-sensitive.** Each handler migration follows the same pattern: replace is_light with workflow_includes_step, replace hardcoded next_step with workflow_next(state)[0], run tests. The key gotcha is handle_critique's synthetic gate.json \u2014 it must still be written for light mode even after removing is_light. The condition flips from `if is_light` to `if not workflow_includes_step(robustness, \"gate\")` which is logically equivalent but reads differently.\n\n**T7 is the payoff.** Once handlers use the workflow, swapping infer_next_steps to alias workflow_next should be a no-op change. If tests break here, something in Phase 2 silently changed state shapes.\n\n**On F003 (over-engineering risk):** The Transition dataclass adds structure but the repo only has two robustness levels. The important thing is that the routing truth lives in one place, not that it uses any particular data structure.\n\n**On the 'step' option:** infer_next_steps currently appends 'step' as a contextual option. workflow_next must replicate this exactly. Check what conditions govern when 'step' appears and ensure those conditions are preserved.\n\n**Testing strategy:** Run `python -m pytest tests/ -x -q` after every task from T3 onward. The -x flag stops on first failure, which is what you want during incremental migration."

_workflow_robustness_from_state(state: PlanState) -> str:\n+    config = state.get(\"config\", {})\n+    if not isinstance(config, dict):\n+        return \"standard\"\n+    return _normalize_workflow_robustness(config.get(\"robustness\", \"standard\"))\n+\n+\n+def _workflow_for_robustness(robustness: str) -> dict[str, list[Transition]]:\n+    normalized = _normalize_workflow_robustness(robustness)\n+    return {\n+        **WORKFLOW,\n+        **WORKFLOW_OVERRIDES.get(normalized, {}),\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def _transition_matches(state: PlanState, condition: str) -> bool:\n+    if condition == \"always\":\n+        return True\n+    gate = state.get(\"last_gate\", {})\n+    if not isinstance(gate, dict):\n+        gate = {}\n+    recommendation = gate.get(\"recommendation\")\n+    if condition == \"gate_unset\":\n+        return not recommendation\n+    if condition == \"gate_iterate\":\n+        return recommendation == \"ITERATE\"\n+    if gate.get(\"passed\", False)\n+    if condition == \"gate_fallback\":\n+        return bool(recommendation) and recommendation != \"ITERATE\" and recommendation != \"ESCALATE\" and not (\n+ in transitions\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def workflow_next(state: PlanState) -> list[str]:\n+    current = state.get(\"current_state\")\n+    if not isinstance(current, str):\n+        return []\n+    workflow = _workflow_for_robustness(_workflow_robustness_from_state(state))\n+    next_steps = [\n+        transition.next_step\n+        for transition in workflow.get(current, [])\n+        if _transition_matches(state, transition.condition)\n+    ]\n+    if current in _STEP_CONTEXT_STATES:\n+        next_steps.append(\"step\")\n+    return next_steps\n+\n+\n+infer_next_steps = workflow_next\n \n \n def require_state(state: PlanState, step: str, allowed: set[str]) -> None:\n*** End Patch\n"

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/_core.py\n@@\n def workflow_includes_step(robustness: str, step: str) -> bool:\n     if step == \"step\":\n         return True\n     workflow = _workflow_for_robustness(robustness)\n     return any(\n@@\n         for transitions in workflow.values()\n         for transition in transitions\n     )\n \n \n+def workflow_transition(state: PlanState, step: str) -> Transition | None:\n+    current = state.get(\"current_state\")\n+    if not isinstance(current, str):\n+        return None\n+    workflow = _workflow_for_robustness(_workflow_robustness_from_state(state))\n+    for transition in workflow.get(current, []):\n+        if transition.next_step == step and _transition_matches(state, transition.condition):\n+            return transition\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def workflow_primary_next(state: PlanState) -> str | None:\n+    current = state.get(\"current_state\")\n+    if not isinstance(current, str):\n+        return None\n+    workflow = _workflow_for_robustness(_workflow_robustness_from_state(state))\n+    for transition in workflow.get(current, []):\n+        if not _transition_matches(state, transition.condition):\n+            continue\n+        if transition.next_state == current:\n+            continue\n+        return transition.next_step\n+    next_steps = workflow_next(state)\n+    return next_steps[0] if next_steps else None\n+\n+\n def workflow_next(state: PlanState) -> list[str]:\n     current = state.get(\"current_state\")\n     if not isinstance(current, str):\n         return []\n*** End Patch\n"
}

    "description": "Design and implement the workflow definition in megaplan/_core.py near existing state constants (~line 600). Add frozen dataclass Transition(next_step, next_state, condition). Define WORKFLOW_OVERRIDES dict keyed by robustness level with only differing edges (light: STATE_CRITIQUED\u2192[Transition('revise', STATE_GATED)], STATE_EXECUTED\u2192[]). Add pure helper workflow_includes_step(robustness, step) -> bool. Implement workflow_next(state) -> list[str] as a pure function reading robustness via state.get('config', {}).get('robustness', 'standard').     "executor_notes": "Implemented a frozen `Transition` dataclass plus `WORKFLOW` and `WORKFLOW_OVERRIDES` in `megaplan/_core.py`, keeping light-mode differences limited to the critiqued and executed states. Added pure helpers `_normalize_workflow_robustness`, `_workflow_robustness_from_state`, `_workflow_for_robustness`, `_transition_matches`, `workflow_includes_step`, and `workflow_next`; none perform I/O or mutate state. `workflow_next` reads robustness via `state.get('config', {})`, falls back to `'standard'` when config is missing or invalid, preserves the existing standard routing semantics for the legacy partial-state cases, and keeps `'step'` as a contextual option instead of hardcoding it into every workflow override. Replaced the old `infer_next_steps` implementation with the thin alias `infer_next_steps = workflow_next`. Verified the module compiles and that targeted assertions cover standard compatibility plus light-mode `critique -> revise` and `execute -> DONE/no review` behavior.",

    ],

      "description": "Audit and document the two actual workflow flows by tracing state transitions through handlers.py for both light and standard robustness. Verify: Light = initialized\u2192plan\u2192critique\u2192revise\u2192finalize\u2192execute\u2192DONE. Standard = initialized\u2192plan\u2192critique\u2192gate\u2192[revise\u2192critique loop]\u2192finalize\u2192execute\u2192review\u2192DONE. Confirm conditional edges (critique\u2192gate vs critique\u2192revise, gate outcomes, revise\u2192critique vs revise\u2192finalize, execute\u2192review vs execute\u2192DONE). Confirm infer_next_steps returns standard-only options with no robustness awareness.",
      "depends_on": [],
      "status": "done",
      "executor_notes": "Traced `handle_plan`, `handle_critique`, `handle_revise`, `handle_gate`, `handle_finalize`, `handle_execute`, and `handle_review` in `megaplan/handlers.py`. Confirmed the normal light flow is `initialized -> plan -> critique -> revise -> finalize -> execute -> DONE`: `handle_critique` skips gate for light by writing a synthetic `gate.json` and setting `last_gate.recommendation = ITERATE`, `handle_revise` moves light directly to `STATE_GATED` and returns `next_step='finalize'`, and `handle_execute` rewrites `STATE_EXECUTED` to `STATE_DONE` for light. Confirmed the normal standard flow is `initialized -> plan -> critique -> gate -> [revise -> critique loop] -> finalize -> execute -> review -> DONE`: critique routes to gate, gate `PROCEED && passed` moves to `STATE_GATED/finalize`, gate `ITERATE` and failed `PROCEED` return to `STATE_CRITIQUED/revise`, and gate `ESCALATE` routes to `override add-note`. Confirmed review has extra non-happy-path branches outside the requested normal flow: blocked review stays in `STATE_EXECUTED` with `next_step='review'`, and `needs_rework` returns `STATE_FINALIZED` with `next_step='execute'`. Confirmed `infer_next_steps` in `megaplan/_core.py` has no robustness awareness because it only reads `current_state` and `last_gate`; for a critiqued state with empty `last_gate` it always returns `['gate', 'step']`, which is standard-only behavior. actual_steps == [\n+        \"plan\",\n+        \"critique\",\n+        \"gate\",\n+        \"revise\",\n+        \"critique\",\n+        \"gate\",\n+        \"finalize\",\n+        \"execute\",\n+        \"review\",\n+    ]\n+\n+\n+def test_workflow_walk_matches_documented_light_flow() -> None:\n+    light_config = {\"config\": {\"robustness\": \"light\"}}\n+    walk = [\n+        ({\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_INITIALIZED, \"last_gate\": {}, **light_config}, \"plan\"),\n+        ({\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_PLANNED, \"last_gate\": {}, **light_config}, \"critique\"),\n+        ({\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_CRITIQUED, \"last_gate\": {}, **light_config}, \"revise\"),\n+        ({\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_GATED, \"last_gate\": {}, **light_config}, \"finalize\"),\n+        ({\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_FINALIZED, \"last_gate\": {}, **light_config}, \"execute\"),\n+    ]\n+\n+    actual_steps: list[str] = []\n+    for state, expected_step in walk:\n+        assert expected_step in workflow_next(state)\n+        actual_steps.append(expected_step)\n+\n+    assert workflow_next({\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_EXECUTED, \"last_gate\": {}, **light_config}) == []\n+    assert actual_steps == [\"plan\", \"critique\", \"revise\", \"finalize\", \"execute\"]\n+\n+\n def test_plan_rerun_keeps_iteration_and_uses_same_iteration_subversion(plan_fixture: PlanFixture) -> None:\n     megaplan.handle_plan(plan_fixture.root, plan_fixture.make_args(plan=plan_fixture.plan_name))\n     megaplan.handle_override(\n         plan_fixture.root,\n         plan_fixture.make_args(plan=plan_fixture.plan_name,
